- **Checks:** `scripts/make-icons.test.ts` holds the committed files to the script (the cut, the two colours and no other, 16/32/48 RGBA in the `.ico`, the tile sizes) and tests the two encoders; `check-frames.mjs` asks the production build for `/icon.svg`, `/favicon.ico`, `/apple-icon.png`, `/opengraph-image`, `/manifest.webmanifest` and its icons, each in its type and size, and reads the links in the heads of `/` and `/goal` (`check-icons.mjs`).

**The flow, end to end, after the audit of Oct 6 (WEB-FLOW-FIXES-1; the report's finding numbers in brackets).**
- **Goal screen.** A chip is read on the click [1]. The income example is whole ("$80,000 for $300 a month of income, 5 years, low risk") and opens with nothing missing [2]. A goal or risk the reader answered with a default of its own, which the words did not say either, is marked "Not said in your goal: I assumed this" until it is changed [3]. The note about the reader and the "parser: …" line are gone; what was not found is named in one line [4]. Signed out, the build button's place holds a primary link, "Sign in to build my plan", and nothing is said as an error (`ConstraintSheet`, `next`) [5]. Goal, time, risk, country and amount are in view; holdings, the glide and the language are folded under "More limits" (`ConstraintSheet`, `more`), which opens itself when a folded field does not fit; the headline is not made from "0 months" [6]. The page moves to the built plan, with the keyboard [15].
- **Plan.** The chips that repeated the heading and the short disclaimer line are gone; how the range was worked out is in Details; a flag this app has no sentence for is one plain line ("The plan carries one more note we can’t describe yet.") [12]. An income plan says what its range a year comes to a month, as an estimate with the pin of the yield reading it stands on ("About $147 to $163 a month if the projected range holds. An estimate, not a promise."), whether or not an income was asked; a gap is said to the cent, with the ways the API gives to close it, each in its sentence, and one button, "Change my limits", which hands the goal screen the limits the plan was built from [11]. The API sends the ways as sentences, so they are not a button each. A plan is kept in the browser (`localStorage`, the eight newest) as a cache, so the screens open at once; the server is the last word (API-PLANS, `GET /v1/baskets/{id}`): a cached plan it says is gone or another person's (404, 403) is dropped and not shown, but only after it was asked once more with fresh tokens (a stale sign-in is answered as nobody is), and never for development's throwaway wallet, which a real server always answers as nobody; one it does not answer about stands, and on sign-out the cache and that person's order records are forgotten in this browser (so a half-done order cannot be resumed from this browser after a sign-out; the trust acceptance stays: it holds no figure; what was signed for a step stays, since it is what stops a second signature). A plan nobody has says so with "Build it again" [14].
- **One name for a token** (`tokenName` and `displayName`, `features/order/amounts.ts` and `plain.ts`), on the plan, the order, the funds step, the portfolio, the vault page, the shelf and the activity: "syrupUSDC (Maple)", "Cash (USDC)", "SPYx". A test network's token goes by the token it stands in for ("tSPYx" is SPYx, "tUSDC" is USDC) and the card says "Test network"; the engine's sentences are passed through the same rule (`plainNames`). Robinhood Chain's dollar keeps the one name it has, tUSDG [13].
- **Buy.** A lone mark in an amount is read as the page's language reads it, so "10.555" in English is refused, not read as $10,555 [18]. An amount that is not the plan's says the plan was built for another [16]. The funds step names cash as the plan does, offers one way to fill the wallet at a time, and writes its source line with the one time format [19]. The short points of the trust notice leave out the keeper's limits for a plan's own vault, which nothing trades (they stay in the full list); a notice accepted before is not a step again, and is still there to read at the review. Accepted means by this person, for this text, in this browser (`tf-trust:<person>` in `localStorage`; the API's consent route is not built): another person, an earlier text or a new device is asked again, and without it the review's button refuses. The acceptance records whether the keeper's limits were shown, so someone who accepted on a plan's buy is asked again on a buy the keeper may trade [20]. Step 4 is "Review", with the same words for a plan and a shared portfolio [21].
- **Order.** A done order offers "See your portfolio" and "Buy more" [22]. A trade is said in the token's own units with the price that minimum means ("receive at least 0.0099 SPYx (at most $606.06 each)"); the mock's tokens have their decimals in `units.ts`, held to `packages/chain-mock` by `tests/web-units.test.ts`, and a token with no units gets how far under the quote the step may land and no raw count [23]. A refusal's check and the guard's words are behind "Details for support"; when a later step fails after the deposit landed, the page says the deposit is in the vault as cash, and leads to the portfolio first, a new order beside it. Where the server has `POST /v1/orders/{id}/continue` the first thing offered is "Finish buying with the cash in your vault": the route is looked for only once an order has stopped, by its status: asked with something that is not an order's id, a server with the route answers 400 and one without answers 404; the button is not there until it answers. The trades the next order is held to are the approved order's own, by step, with only each step's standing read from the API. The order it makes is kept with the trades the first one left, and is offered for signing only if it deposits nothing, opens nothing, says which order it finishes, and its trades are those trades (`checkContinuation`, `order-check.ts`) [24]. The order page has no disclaimer block of its own: the shell's foot draws it, which the order screen's test holds in both languages. Every time is "Oct 5, 2026, 15:00 UTC" (`features/portfolio/figures.ts`, `utc`) [25]. The step that opens the vault and buys is called that; "as our server reports it" is gone [26]. The order's page no longer lists its steps a second time; the portfolio lists what was done under the order it was a step of [27].
- **Portfolio and vault.** Cash is a holding row, so the shares add up to 100 (`holdingsOf`); the table is "What you hold", with "Share now", "Planned" and "Difference", to one decimal at most, rounded together so a column adds up to 100.0 (`sharesOf`), with each row's difference worked from the two shares as written; the keeper's losses keep two decimals [28, 32]. The chips, the auto-follow and keeper tiles and "No status yet: the engine gives none" are gone from the first view: the vault's address, the version it follows, auto-follow and, with auto-follow on, the keeper's losses are behind "Details", and the goal card says "Goal date: October 2029" or the income verdict [31]. The goal card leads with what was put in when that is not the plan's amount, says what a vault bought from a shared portfolio follows, and its quiet line is in the language of the view [30, 32, 33]. The vault's address leads to its page, which leads back and to the explorer, counts cash as a holding and writes its figures as the portfolio does [34, 35]. When the chain the bar is on holds no vault and another does, the page says so first and names the chain of each vault [36]; "No wallet of this sign-in is on …" is said only where there is none [37]. A link drawn as a button goes through the router inside the shell (`components/shell/RouterLinks.tsx`) [33].
- **The rest.** A passkey that is not taken says so, and "Create a new passkey" says it opens a new, empty wallet [39]. On a shared portfolio's page a vault bought from a goal goes by that goal, and the creator's address and the routine text check are behind Details; where the version and weights come from stays in view [41]. Findings 34, 38 and 42 (the link to the vault's page, the shelf's sentence, the publish form) came with `WEB-FLOW-FIXES-3`; this branch keeps them as they are.
- **Not here:** the test tokens' issuers and tiers and the credit budget [8, 10], test funds on the hosted API [17], plans, goals and history kept on the server [14, 30], shared portfolios published [40], withdrawing [29], and the country asked at sign-in [7].

**Why a plan holds what it holds (WEB-PLAN-BINDING, from Thom's own buy of Oct 6: $80,000 for $300 a month came out 25% syrupUSDC and 75% cash, beside "starts with 100% in dollar yield").**
- **The reason that decided a line** (`bindingReason` in `features/order/plain.ts`), in the engine's own sentence: the credit limit (`CREDIT_BUDGET*`), the cap on a token or an issuer (`ASSET_CAP`, `ISSUER_CAP*`, `SINGLE_STOCK_CAP`), the exit ceiling (`EXIT_CEILING`, `TIER_CEILING`); on cash, why money stayed (`UNPLACED`, `NO_DOLLAR_YIELD`, `YIELD_TOO_SMALL`, what is set aside). Where a line only started (`SLEEVE*`, `CASH_NEAR_DATE`, `GLIDE`) is said only when nothing else is. The legs show that reason first, and the summary sentence says it for the largest holding and for cash, so it never states a share the lines contradict (a test scans every share and amount in it).
- **What was left out** (`removed[]`) is listed in Details, each in the engine's sentence, and money that could not be placed is said by the cash line's own reason in place of the general note.
- **An income goal says its income:** "Earn $300 a month from $80,000 for 12 months.", on the plan, the goal screen's card and the vault's goal card.
- **On the portfolio** cash is a part of the plan like the others ("Cash (USDC) 75%", counted in "Your plan · 2 parts", with its segment in the bar), and the parts and the holdings table use the plan screen's names ("syrupUSDC (Maple)"). A short income plan's status is in figures: "When this plan was built, it paid $71.77 a month of the $300 you asked for." (`GoalCard`'s status takes no date after a whole sentence).

**The plan in plain words (WEB-PLAN-CLARITY, Thom, Oct 6).** The plan screen showed engine codes and ids. Now (`features/order/plain.ts`):
- **No code on the page.** Each flag of the engine and the roll-up is a sentence in the dictionary (`plan.flagWords`, English and Portuguese), each once; a flag no sentence names is said by one sentence that there is a note, never by its code. Kinds of asset are words (`plan.kinds`). The notes and the spread of the plan ("How the plan is spread, and what selling costs", was "Risk, as our server rolled it up") are in a closed "Details" under the plan.
- **Names, not ids.** "syrupUSDC (Maple)", "Cash (USDC)", "SPYx": the shelf's symbols, the issuer where it says what a dollar-yield token is, a test network's token by the token it stands in for. The web cannot read the registry (`tests/boundaries.test.ts`), so the symbols are a list in `plain.ts`; an unknown token goes by its symbol in capitals, never its id.
- **One sentence at the top**, from the lines: "$200 for 2 months, high risk, on Solana: $150 stays in Cash (USDC) and $50 goes to syrupUSDC (Maple)." and the largest holding's own reason. No rate in it: the plan carries no rate per line, and a rate needs its pin.
- **A short or flat plan** (under 6 months, or a projection under 1% of the amount) is said in a sentence on the yield's pin ("In 2 months: about $201.50 to $201.60", to the cent where the dollars round the same) instead of a flat chart; the chart's ticks are one per dollar label.
- **A bad fall** is a sentence: "In a bad fall: you'd lose about $0, since nothing here is stocks, crypto or gold.", or the engine's estimate.
- **Punctuation:** the basis sentence keeps one full stop ("gold.." came from the engine's own); a leg with no rate shows no "· —".
- The MOCK plates are left to `web/mock-quiet` (build-3d).

**His partner embed (WEB-EMBED).** `embed-shell.md` and guidelines.html section 08 ("The joints stay. The wood becomes theirs."), in `app/(embed)/` and `features/embed/`.

- **A bare root.** `app/(embed)/layout.tsx` is a document of its own: the stylesheet, the language, and nothing else of ours. No bar, no wallet provider, no account, no font: a test of the import graph holds it (`product-routes.test.ts`), and the e2e finds no `wallet-adapter`, `Newsreader` or wood hex in the page, nor a nav or main landmark (the host owns those).
- **The partner's skin, from the frame's address.** A frame does not inherit its host's CSS, so the host names its colours in the URL: `/embed?fg=&bg=&muted=&border=&accent=&radius=&button=&font=&mono=&scheme=` (`features/embed/theme.ts`). Each is taken only as an opaque `#rrggbb` colour, a length (or `pill` for the button), or a list of font names; anything else is dropped, and the system's colours stand in (`Canvas`, `CanvasText`, and the text mixed toward the ground for the muted colour, since `GrayText` on white is 3.9:1). Anyone can write that address on our origin, so the colours have a floor (PR #83 review): the ground and the text colour are taken together or not at all, and only at 4.5:1 or more; the muted colour and the accent are taken only at 4.5:1 on the ground, and the muted colour is the text colour when none is taken. So the disclaimer, the pins, the hatch and MOCK cannot be made invisible from the URL, and `embed-shell.md`'s low-contrast case (drop the hatch, keep MOCK) cannot arise: a faint muted colour is refused instead. The shell draws the partner's container as the guide does: their ground, hairline, radius and 1.25em inside.
- **`/embed`: the goal, then its limits, then out.** A typing box and a button in the partner's skin (not our rounded composer, which the spec says does not appear), read by the public reader (`POST /goals`, no sign-in); the limits as rows; then "Build this plan in tenonfi", a link that opens `/goal` in a new tab with the goal in the address's fragment, which no server sees, and `/goal` reads it into its box. Any site can link to `/goal#goal=<text>` the same way; it only fills the box and is read by the public reader, and nothing is built or signed until the person asks. A plan is not built inside the frame: building one needs the person's sign-in (`POST /v1/baskets/personalize`), and nothing is signed there. The disclaimer and the credit ("Powered by", the symbol and the word, in the partner's muted colour, linking to tenonfi in a new tab) close it.
- **`/embed/[chain]/[address]`: a vault, read only.** The public vault route (`GET /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}`, WEB-4): its value and each part on a pin, the hatch and one quiet line ("Sample figures · test network") where not live, and a link to the public vault page; an answer for another vault, or none, is one sentence.
- **Framing.** `lib/frame-policy.ts`, through `next.config.ts`: the embed's two pages, `/embed` and `/embed/<chain>/<address>`, answer `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'self'` plus the https origins in `EMBED_FRAME_ANCESTORS` (a deployment's list of partners, space separated); every other address answers `frame-ancestors 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `/embed/x` and `/embed/a/b/c` included, since they fall to the `(structurer)` page for an unknown address, which has a wallet button. Next matches a header's source without regard to case while its routes keep it, so `/Embed` would take the embed's policy on that same page; `proxy.ts`, which every spelling of `/embed` reaches, holds any address that is not exactly one of the two to nobody. `check-build.mjs` starts the production build and asks 13 addresses who may frame them (`check-frames.mjs`). The list is read when the app is built (it is written into `routes-manifest.json`), so naming a partner takes a rebuild. No partner is named yet, so on a deployment only the app itself can frame the embed until one is.
- **Height.** The embed posts `{ type: 'tenonfi:embed-height', height }` to its parent whenever its height changes (`host-height.ts`), to `*`: it carries a number and nothing else, and who may frame it is the CSP's to say. A host checks `event.source` is its frame and sets the frame's height. A host whose listener comes up after the first message (a race the e2e met) asks with `{ type: 'tenonfi:embed-height-request' }` sent to the frame, and the embed answers its parent alone.
- **`/dev/embed`** is a stand-in host: four frames (the system's light and dark, his sample partner in light and dark from `.partner-app`), each sized by the height it posts; `?width=375` narrows them, `?vault=solana/<address>` frames a vault.
- **Differences from section 08** (screenshots at 375 and 1280 beside it): his card shows a goal already built (progress, a pinned rate, a MOCK leg, "See your plan"); this one shows the goal being read, since a plan needs sign-in, and the vault view carries the pins and MOCK. His disclaimer box has an 8px radius under a 14px card; ours takes the partner's one radius. His sample's base text is 15px; ours is the host's (16px in the system's).
 Under `apps/web/features/shared/`, on the primitives, in both languages, light and dark. Publishing and following are orders, reviewed and signed on the order screen (`/orders/[id]`) through `execute` of `@colosseum/sdk`, as a buy is; nothing else signs.

- **The shelf** (`/shelf`, in the bar). `GET /v1/shelf?chain=` with the person's chain (gate `ONE-CHAIN`; signed out, every chain). A card per portfolio: its name as the card's one link, the creator's address, the platform badge, the chains, the version in effect and its weights on one line, whether auto-follow is offered (a word and a shape), and the version that waits. The MOCK plate on a portfolio of the mock or a test network.
- **A portfolio's page** (`/indexes/[slug]`). Its name and description, the creator, and the recipe of the person's chain: the version in effect as the plan pane's legs (a table beyond four assets), the version that waits with its time, where the version and weights came from, and the auto-follow offer. `GET /v1/indexes/{slug}/versions` as a table. Signed in: "Buy and follow this portfolio", and the person's vaults on their chain (`GET /v1/portfolio`): a vault that follows it shows the prompt when a newer version is in effect (accept it) or waits (its time), and the auto-follow switch only where the portfolio offers it, the one-tap sentence otherwise (gate `GOLD-ONE-TAP`); a vault that follows something else, "Follow with this vault". Each is a `follow` order.
- **Buy and follow** (`/indexes/[slug]/buy`). The buy screen's four steps: amount, funding (`GET /v1/funding` with `family`) and trust notice, then a `buy` with `family` and the version shown.
- **Publish** (`/publish`). Name, address on the shelf (made from the name until edited), description, and three to twelve assets with weights (the committed deployment's assets; on the mock, the mock's shelf, MOCK). The family id is worked out on the page from the address (`familyIdOf`) and shown; an address the person published before is the next version (`update`), another creator's is refused. The four limits are checked on the page (count, 2% to 50% in 0.5% steps, 100%, each asset once) and again by the server and the registry. The order is a `publish` with the form's id and text.
- **The public vault page** (`/vaults/[chain]/[address]`). `GET /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}` (new, public): owner, what it follows and at which version, auto-follow, value, and each position with its held amount, price (pinned), weight, target and drift. Nothing is signed or written. Anybody may ask, so the API keeps an answer for 10 seconds per chain and address (`VAULT_KEPT_MS`, in memory, 500 addresses at most): a link opened by many reads the chain once in that time.
- **What a signature is held to** (`features/shared/terms.ts`). The screen that makes an order keeps its terms beside it in the order record (`order-record.ts`): for a publish the form's family id, text, weights and version; for a buy of a portfolio and a follow, the portfolio's account, version and weights. The order screen hands them to the runner, which makes the guard's `PlanTerms` (`planTermsOf` in `run-order.ts`); the API's order is never read for them. Before offering to sign, the order is held to them (`order-check.ts`): a buy of a portfolio deposits what was typed and spends it on the weights read, split as the API splits it (`tradesOf`); a publish has one `publish` step; a follow only the accept and the switch its terms call for. A follow names a vault by its plan number only when that number derives the vault's address (`vaultOf` of the SDK).
- **The gap API-3 left, closed where it can be.** A follow's terms are read from the chain by the browser, through this app's own node (`NEXT_PUBLIC_CHAIN_READ_RPC_SOLANA`, the node the executor reads, `features/order/chain-node.ts`): `readSolanaRecipe` of the SDK reads the registry's account at the address it derives from the creator and the family id the slug gives, with the cluster's clock, and maps each mint to an asset by the committed deployment. What it reads is what the page shows and what the buy or follow is held to; where our server answered something else the page says so as an alarm. Where it cannot read, the page says which, and shows our server's answer as not checked: the mock (there is no chain), no node set for this app, no deployment committed for the network, a portfolio whose id is not its slug's (not published through the app: the slug-to-id link is the server's word; not offered for following), or an EVM chain (its registry read waits for ADE-2). Where it has a node and a deployment and the read fails (the node did not answer, the account is not the program's recipe of that creator and family, the server named a creator that is no Solana address), nothing is offered: no buy and no follow, with the sentence that the portfolio could not be verified and may have been tampered with (the review of #62: a server that lies could otherwise switch the read off). A line whose token the app does not list is shown by its mint, in the version in effect and the one that waits. The creator address the account is derived with is still the server's word: the page shows it in full.
- **The guard on the mock's publish.** `packages/sdk/src/guard/mock/check.ts` holds a mock publish to the step as the Solana check does: the creator, the family, the hash of the form's text worked out by the guard, the assets and weights, no fee and no flags. The mock has one operation for a first version and the next, so `publish` and `update` are not told apart there; a cancel is refused as `unsupported`.
- **The creator's words** are text everywhere: React writes them as text, never as markup or a link. Where the chain was read, the page works out `familyTextHash` of the name and description it shows and marks them "can't vouch for them" when no version on the chain carries that hash; where nothing was read it says the text is not checked, and does not repeat the server's `textMatches`. The publish form refuses what the server refuses (a link, a bare web address or an email, a hidden character), and updates only a portfolio whose stored id is `familyIdOf(slug)`. An accept never asks to keep auto-follow on where the portfolio does not offer it. The public vault page pins the vault's value as the monitor does (`vaultValueSource`). The creator's address is shown in full, from the chain's account where read; the platform badge only for a creator in `features/shared/platform.ts` (empty until the launch portfolios are published from a platform key), never from the server's `platform`. A portfolio whose id is not `familyIdOf(slug)` is not offered for following (gate `FAMILY-ID`), and every follow names `basketIdOfPlan(familyIdOf(slug))`.
- **Checks.** `features/shared/shared-order.events.test.ts` (6: the executor is handed the form's publish terms, the followed version, the vault's plan number; an order with a step its terms do not call for, or trades on other weights, is shown and not offered), `shared-screens.events.test.ts` (9: the shelf asks for the person's chain and shows markup as text; the switch only where offered, the one-tap sentence otherwise; the prompt's accept; a vault whose plan number is not its own; another chain's portfolio; a buy's version and kept terms; the publish form's id, limits and text), `chain-read.events.test.ts` (8: a node that holds another version and weights than the server: the page shows the chain's and says so, and the buy asks for and keeps the chain's account, version and weights; the text's hash; an unlisted line that waits, by its mint; a read that fails with a node set blocks the buy on the page and on the buy screen, for an EVM creator, an account the program does not own and an account of another creator; a portfolio the chain does not hold). `apps/web/e2e/shared.spec.ts`: a publish through the form, signed through the real guard on the mock, the shelf, the page, a buy that follows it, signed, the vault and its public page; a portfolio with gold offers no auto-follow. axe at 375 px in light and dark on every screen, no violation. The stub API (`tests/e2e/stub-api.ts`) serves the shared routes and orders on its mock chain, gold there with no oracle.
- **Differences from his references** (screenshots at 375 and 1280 px, both themes, beside `guidelines.html` (components, legbar, provenance) and `prototypes/hero-3d.html` (the case's plan pane, the closing section)). The shelf's card is the guide's goal card turned into a list card: a sans title, no serif line inside it, the weights as one mono line rather than a bar. The portfolio's page is the plan pane without its chips and stat cells, since a portfolio has no goal, amount or horizon; its legs carry a dash for the yield they do not state. The buy and the review are WEB-3's. The publish form is the guide's controls (fields and secondary buttons) in two cards; there is no reference for an editable list of weights. The public vault page is a stat row and a table, as the monitor's. None of his references is a shared-portfolio screen: these are the nearest.
- **Not built.** The follow prompt on the monitor (WEB-2's screen, not edited here: `FollowPrompt` is exported for it), a cancel of a version that waits, a read of an EVM recipe from this app's own node (on Robinhood Chain a follow shows the server's answer as not checked, with its own reason, `no-reader`: this app does not read that registry yet, whether or not a node is set), and value following on the shelf. The pages read on the client, not statically generated with `revalidate: 60` as section 11 sets for the shelf and a portfolio's page: they hang on the person's chain and on reads of the chain made by the browser.

**Waiting for data (WEB-LOADING, Oct 6, Thom).** The hosted API sleeps on Render's free plan and can take up to a minute to wake, so every screen that reads waits in the shape of what comes: `components/ui/Skeleton.tsx` draws still boxes in the layout's own grid (a figure, a line, a stat row, table rows, a chart frame, the plan pane, a list of cards), and `Waiting.tsx` puts them in a region that is `aria-busy`, with one polite status line under them. The spec has no shimmer and nothing ambient (card.md, button.md): the boxes do not pulse, and the one moving thing is STYLE.md's loader, the lattice that assembles, shown with its words only once a wait passes 400ms and still under reduced motion. After 4 seconds the line adds "Waking the data service: this can take up to a minute the first time"; after a minute (`wait.ts`) the wait gives up with a sentence and a retry. Bearing's pages wait in their own boxes: the selectors, the row of figures with their labels and their notes laid out unseen under their bars, the two chart cards and the table, so the row of figures does not move when the data comes (`e2e/bearing.spec.ts` holds its box to the pixel); its banner keeps its height with still bars. Every other screen's card waits in its shape (`components/shell/Wait.tsx`), the goal screen shows the outcome card while a plan is built, the partner embed keeps still boxes and words only (embed-shell.md), and the pages made on the server have a `loading.tsx` of the same shapes.

**The design system in the app (BRAND-1).** `/dev/ui` under `pnpm dev` shows every primitive in every state, in light beside dark, under the name of its spec, with made-up content labelled MOCK. It is a development route (`app/(app)/dev/ui/page.dev.tsx`, under the product's layout since WEB-1): a production build has no such route, and `scripts/check-build.mjs` fails one that does.

- **Tokens:** `apps/web/app/globals.css`, in the three layers of his `token-mapping.md`: the semantic variables on `:root` and `.dark` (and `.light`, so a light sample can sit in a dark page), the `--tf-*` extensions, and the Tailwind theme. `components/ui/tokens.test.ts` compares every value with `working-brand.yml`.
- **Fonts:** `apps/web/app/fonts.ts` loads Newsreader, IBM Plex Sans and IBM Plex Mono with `next/font/local` from files committed under `apps/web/assets/fonts` (the Latin files Google Fonts serves, and the Greek file of IBM Plex Sans for the letters Bearing writes, byte for byte, with each face's OFL beside them; `app/fonts.test.ts` pins their hashes), so a build fetches nothing: until Oct 6 `next/font/google` fetched them at build, and CI failed whenever it could not reach Google. They are served from our origin, each with a fallback whose metrics match, written in `globals.css` with the figures the Google build worked out. The product's layout preloads the sans and the serif (WEB-1); the mono face is in `app/fonts-mono.ts`, not preloaded, and it is the only one his pages load. IBM Plex Sans Condensed is not loaded; `font-condensed` is IBM Plex Sans until a Bearing screen loads it.
- **The pages that exist look as they did.** The base styles of the spec (ground, ink, face, tabular figures, focus ring) sit under one class, `tf-app`, not on `html` and `body`. WEB-1 put `tf-app` on the product's `<body>` and deleted the two lines that kept Tailwind's base fonts; his layout carries `tf-system-faces` instead, which names the same system faces, so his pages read as they did. One thing did change in BRAND-1: text with the `font-mono` class is in IBM Plex Mono, because the class reads the token.
- **Primitives** are in `apps/web/components/ui/`, each with a test beside it:

| His spec | File | What it gives |
|---|---|---|
| `button.md` | `Button.tsx`, `button-class.ts` | `Button`: primary, secondary, link, chip, icon, destructive; dense; busy; disabled. Busy or disabled, it cancels the click, so it cannot submit a form or be followed as a link |
| `field.md` | `Field.tsx` | `Field`, `Input`, `Select`, `Textarea` |
| `card.md` | `Card.tsx` | `Card`, `CardHeader`, `CardBody`, `CardFooter`, `Stat`, `StatRow`, `CardLoading`, `CardEmpty`. `Card mock` draws the hatch band and the plate itself |
| `provenance-pin.md` | `ProvenancePin.tsx`, `provenance.ts`, `price-source.ts` | `ProvenancePin` (the figure, the glyph, the popover); `pinState` and the `PinSource` type; `pinSourceOfPrice`, which turns a `Price` into what the pin takes |
| `mock-plate.md` | `MockPlate.tsx`, `internal/mock-parts.tsx` | `MockPlate` (always the band and the plate), `MockFrame`, `StalePlate`. The band alone and the plate alone are in `internal/`, for the primitives only |
| `disclaimer-block.md` | `Disclaimer.tsx` | `Disclaimer`, which takes a language and no text |
| `data-table.md` | `DataTable.tsx`, `ExecutionList.tsx`, `ExplorerLink.tsx`, `CopyButton.tsx` | the table, the execution list, the `Tx` link |
| `goal-card.md` | `GoalCard.tsx` | `GoalCard`, as a card and as the plan view's header |
| `plan-leg.md` | `PlanLegs.tsx` | `PlanLegs`; a fifth leg throws |
| `exit-plan-line.md` | `ExitPlanLine.tsx` | `ExitPlanLine` (tiers), `ExitPlanPanel` ("Access to cash") |
| `constraint-sheet.md` | `ConstraintSheet.tsx` | `ConstraintSheet`, to edit and to read; `facts` for what it states and does not edit (WEB-1) |
| `composer.md` | `Composer.tsx`, `composer-keys.ts` | `Composer`, on several lines or one |
| `compact-nav.md` | `CompactNav.tsx` | `CompactNav` |
| `embed-shell.md` | `EmbedShell.tsx`, `tf-embed` in `globals.css` | `EmbedShell` |
| `subscribe-block.md` | `SubscribeBlock.tsx` | `SubscribeBlock` |
| `STYLE.md`, `iconography.md` | `StatusMark.tsx`, `Icon.tsx`, `Lattice.tsx` | the status mark with its word, the icon wrapper, the still lattice for a wait or an empty state |

- **Not built:** the `code` placement of the MOCK plate (the API serves its own docs), the assembling loader (the primitives use the still lattice their specs ask for), and the restyle of the wallet adapter: the product's routes do not mount it at all (WEB-1), and it leaves with his pages (WEB-2). `goal-showcase-case.md` and `joint-stage.md` are built as the landing page's own parts (WEB-2b, `features/landing/`), not as primitives. The heatmap tile was built with Bearing's analytics (WEB-BEARING): a cell with no sample is drawn as its spec says, the ground and an en dash.
- **What a figure is handed.** A yield, a price or an FX figure is shown through `ProvenancePin`, which takes `source`, `fetchedAt`, `method` and `provenance` (the `Sourced` shape) and `staleAgeSec`. Without a source and a method that are more than spaces, and a time that is a date-time with a zone, it shows a dash and "no source yet", never the figure. Only the word `live` is live: every other provenance, one this build does not know included, is drawn as MOCK. Stale is what the API states; the pin never works it out from the time. For a price a screen calls `pinSourceOfPrice(price)`: `staleAgeSec` is the price's own `ageSeconds` when `isStalePrice` says it is older than `maxAgeSeconds` (3.1), and null otherwise. A yield, a quote or a preview has no age yet, so it is never drawn stale until `Sourced` gets its staleness field. An age that is not a number of seconds (`NaN`, below zero) is shown as "stale · age unknown".
- **Words.** The few words a primitive says itself (the pin's name, "stale", "failed (not retried)") are English defaults, and every primitive takes them back through a `labels` prop, for the dictionary. The defaults of the client components are in `labels.ts`, a plain module: a file marked `'use client'` exports components and types only, so a server component never reads a value from one.
- **What throws.** A fifth plan leg, a stock token in an income plan, and a status with no word (`Status`, the goal card, a table row): each is an error upstream, and the screen fails instead of showing it.

**Where his files disagreed or left a choice, and the reading taken.** Any of these is his to reopen.

1. The pin's glyph is drawn from `provenance-pin.md` and the `.yml`: an 18 by 12 box, 0.75em tall, a stone outline, the pin in the brand wood, a 3px hatch, a hollow ring as wide as the pin. `logo-directions.md` (a 12 by 12 box, a 1.25 stroke) and `typography.md` (`1cap`) say otherwise.
2. The words beside a hollow pin ("stale · 3 h") are caption text in the sans face, as `provenance-pin.md` says; `typography.md` and `guidelines.html` set stale labels in mono.
3. A figure from a test network (`sandbox`) carries the hatched glyph like any other figure that is not live. Since gate `MOCK-QUIET` (Thom, Oct 6) no figure carries the boxed word MOCK: a sample card keeps its hatched edge and says so once, in a muted line at its foot ("Sample figures · test network"); a sample figure keeps its hatched pin or a small hatched glyph, named "sample figure" for a screen reader; live figures keep their pin. The words "test network" are in that line, in the pin's popover, and after the glyph in a row of the execution list. A chain's name takes the glyph, then "test network" in words (`features/account/ChainName.tsx`).
4. The popover is a tooltip, or a dialog when it holds a link, as the spec says. Its source line is a button in both, because the spec asks that a click on it copies the line.
5. The popover is placed against the window, not inside its parent, so a table that scrolls cannot cut it off.
6. The disclaimer's box is a hairline (`--border`), as `disclaimer-block.md` says; `guidelines.html` draws it in member.
7. In the embed, mono text takes `--embed-mono` and falls back to the system's monospace (`token-mapping.md` section 8, `embed-shell.md`); `typography.md` names `--tf-mono` and falls back to Plex Mono. The shell takes `--embed-radius` or inherits; boxes inside it are square when the partner sets none, and the partner's buttons take `--embed-button-radius` (a pill in his sample) or the boxes' radius. A status keeps its word and shape and takes the partner's text colour.
8. IBM Plex Sans Condensed counts as IBM Plex Sans for the rule of three typefaces: `typography.md` says "three faces, two families".
9. The MOCK badge has 6px between band and plate, as in `guidelines.html`. A stale plate is the same box in sentence case.
10. The plan-lock is the recipe of STYLE.md: the legs seat one after another, 60ms apart, then the pins drop. A leg's "why" line is always shown unless the screen drives "Why this plan?", when it shows only while the bar is parted.
11. A busy button keeps its fill and changes its label. A month on the sheet is a plain `YYYY-MM` field, the same in every browser.
12. The plain names of the shadow, ring, blur, italic and light-weight utilities are never generated by Tailwind (a blocklist in `globals.css`), so a word in a comment cannot make one. The same utilities behind a variant or with a value in brackets are still generated when a file asks for them; `forbidden.test.ts` is what fails then. Bare `rounded` is still 4px, because the pages not yet rebuilt use it; when they are, `--radius` goes into the theme.

**Where his specs change what we build.**

- **The goal comes first.** The home screen is the composer, not the shelf. A shared portfolio is reached from the plan or the shelf and always leads back to the goal screen.
- **At most four legs in the bar.** His plan-leg spec treats more as an engine error. The bar shows the plan's sleeves (stocks, dollar yield, gold, cash). The tokens inside each sleeve go in a data table under it.
- **Status on the goal card.** "On track", "Watch" or "Off track" comes from the server, never the UI. The engine computes a verdict for income goals only, so for every goal the status is the rule `ON-TRACK-V1` (`docs/GATES.md`, Oct 6), a first version: worked out from the newest snapshot of the plan's vault and printed with the line that gave it, never a probability (PORT-2). The engine's verdict takes its place through the same function when ENG-3 lands. The odds his showcase prints stay open (section 17).
- **The exit plan is tiers in time,** such as "up to $4,000 within a day, the rest within 7 days, cost ≤ 0.50%", shown before the invest button, with costs as "≤" or "about" and never as a promise. The roll-up in `packages/basket` gives a cost at a size; it also has to give tiers. Withdrawing the tokens themselves is stated on its own line and is not called access to cash.
- **Test networks.** A figure whose provenance is `sandbox` (a test network, or a local copy of mainnet) is shown with the mock-plate treatment, the hatch, and the words "test network" where a mock says MOCK. It is never shown as live.
- **Pins need staleness.** The pin has a stale state that the API must state; the UI may not infer it. A price states it already: it carries its own age and the chain's limit (`isStalePrice`, 3.1). `Sourced` gets a staleness field at the first interface freeze, agreed with Rodrigo (his own open item).
- **Signing.** A button that signs names the action and the amount ("Sign: swap 5 USDC → USDY"), and there is one primary per view. A failed mainnet line has no retry button and reads "(not retried)"; a new attempt is a new action the person signs. A busy button changes its label; there are no spinners.
- **The agent's log speaks in the first person with its reason and its source:** "I moved $1,200 from USDC to USDY because rates dipped and your June date needs a little more income. Source · Tx ↗". Every keeper leg stores the reason it was made, so the line can be written.
- **Wallets.** His specs style the Solana wallet-adapter button and modal, and his landing page says "Connect wallet". Passkey sign-in through Privy needs the same care. The button says "Sign in" and opens a choice of passkey or wallet (section 17). As built (WEB-1) it is a link in the bar to `/sign-in`, a screen of ours on the primitives; nothing of Privy's is drawn.
- **Languages.** Strings live in a dictionary keyed by language from day one, with human labels for every sheet field. English and Portuguese both, since his home screen is Portuguese today. As built (WEB-1): `apps/web/i18n/`, above; the primitives take their words through `labels`.

- **Order executor.** The web uses `execute()` from `packages/sdk` with a `WalletPort`-backed signer; there is one state machine, not two. It writes every transition to the API before the next step. One run of an order at a time: the caller holds a lock named for the order around `execute` (in a browser `navigator.locks`, across its tabs), since two runs could each sign a step before either writes down what it signed. On reload, `sent` legs are tracked; a `built` leg whose signature was never reported is reported again, and any other `built` or `expired` leg is rebuilt and signed again only as the as-built text below allows. A failed leg stops its chain only. Status changes go to an `aria-live` region with the explorer link.
  - As built (AGT-1): `execute(order, { api, signer, deployments, plan, consents, signed, chainRead, approvedAgain })`. `order` is the order as the review screen showed it; what each step may sign is worked out from it once, and the API's later answers are read only for what has happened. For each step it builds, runs the guard, signs, reports and waits for the chain, one step at a time. The wallet is asked only for what the guard passed, and what a wallet that signs hands back is read before it is reported: the same message (Solana) or the same call (EVM). One approved step gets one signature: what was signed is kept by order and step (`signed`, a store that outlives the page; nothing is signed without one) and reported again instead of made again, and bytes signed for one step are never signed for another. A second signature for the same step needs the chain's word that the first can no longer land, read through the caller's own connection to one node (`chainRead`, made with `chainReadOf({ solana, evm })`, each one `RpcCall` such as `rpcAt(url)`), or the person approving the step again. On Solana, before each signature, the node must have the blockhash the transaction is bound to (`isBlockhashValid` at `processed`); its block height then is kept with the signature, and the first is gone only once the node's finalized block height is more than 150 blocks plus a margin of 30 past it and the chain has no transaction with that signature (`getSignatureStatuses`, history searched, so the node has to keep transaction history: a node that keeps only recent slots answers not found for one that landed, and a pruned answer is taken as gone). Whether the blockhash is still valid is never the test: a node answers no for a blockhash it has not seen yet, and the finalized block trails the tip by some 32 slots. On a deployment that is the mock no height is asked: the mock's transactions have no blockhash and carry their lifetime in the mock's own clock. A node that does not have the blockhash after a few tries, or does not answer, means nothing is signed: the attempt is closed and the executor answers `waiting` (`unknown_blockhash`). On EVM the first is gone once the finalized nonce of the account has moved past it and the chain has no such transaction. The reads of one chain come from one node: a URL that balances calls across nodes can answer one read from a node that is behind and the next from one that is ahead. When the chain cannot tell, the person decides: the executor then answers `needs_review`, and signs once more only when it is run with `approvedAgain` set from that answer. The API's word that a step did not land is never enough, and without `chainRead` a step is not signed twice. A guard refusal and a wallet failure both cancel the attempt at the API before the executor answers. It answers `done` (the API's word that every step is confirmed), `refused` (with the `GuardRefusal`), `cancelled` (with the wallet's code), `failed` (a revert, which is never sent again), `expired`, `blocked` (EVM: another order of the wallet holds a transaction that can still land, named as the API names it), `needs_review`, `waiting` (nothing wrong; run it again) or `error`. A report the chain has not seen yet is sent again; a step whose transaction can no longer land is built again, twice at most, and an API that opens a new attempt each time one is closed is followed only as far. One prompt signs one step: the API builds a step only when the one before it has settled, so the three-at-a-time signing of an outside Solana wallet is not used.
- **Checks.** Every Playwright spec runs axe at 375 px in light and dark. A DOM test fails the build if a hatch appears without words that say sample (the card's line, a named glyph or pin) or "stale" in the same component, or if the boxed word MOCK is drawn (`MOCK-QUIET`). A CI grep rejects raw palette classes and any blue. An unknown provenance never renders as live. BRAND-1 wrote the ones that need no browser, in `apps/web/components/ui/`: `forbidden.test.ts` (in the source and in the compiled stylesheet: a hue between 200° and 330° in OKLCH with a chroma of 0.008 or more, a shadow, a corner that is not 0 or 2px outside the composer, a typeface outside the three, uppercase outside the MOCK plate, italic, a weight under 400, a gradient outside the hatch, a blur or a backdrop filter, centred text outside the two places a spec allows it; the pages written before the design system are listed by name, and the list fails when an entry no longer matches). It does not find the rest of what `STYLE.md` forbids: the other colour families, pure white or black grounds, patterns behind text, the serif where it does not belong, motion that bounces, the icons on the list, anything about words. `hatch.test.ts` holds the hatch rule both ways, a hatch with no word and a MOCK plate with no hatch, on every primitive and on the showcase (`test/hatch.ts` gives it to a screen's own test), and fails when a file outside `components/ui` imports half of the mark or writes its class. Then `contrast.test.ts` (WCAG AA for 80 token pairs in light and dark), `tokens.test.ts`, `shipped.test.ts` (nothing shipped imports the showcase or its sample content, and `scripts/check-build.mjs` refuses a route built from them), `server-safe.test.ts` (a primitive a server component can render calls nothing from a client module, and a client module exports components and types only), and one test per primitive. Most read the markup React's server renderer writes. The guards are tested with real events, in the `*.events.test.ts` files: a busy or disabled button in a form, the sheet's build with and without the button's own guard, the composer while empty, busy or disabled, the pin's Escape, outside press, hover and focus, the nav's sheet. Those run in happy-dom, a development dependency. The two checks that read the output of `next build` are skipped by a plain test run; `scripts/check-build.mjs`, the last step of the web build, runs that file again, and there a missing or stale build fails. WEB-1 added the tests of the screens, with events in happy-dom: `components/shell/` (the shell, the theme, what a product route may import and call), `features/wallet/SignIn.events.test.ts` and `sign-in.test.ts` (each failure and its sentence), `features/account/` (the pick: asked once for a wallet made here, never for a connected one, never stored without a choice), `features/goal/` (nothing built from limits that do not parse, the double of the personalize route), and `i18n/dictionary.test.ts` (the voice rules on every sentence, in both languages). WEB-3 added the first Playwright spec, with axe on the plan, buy and order screens at 375 px in light and dark (above). WEB-2 added `features/portfolio/` (the read and each failure, what every pin is handed, the monitor and home with events), and home and the monitor to that spec: after the buy, the monitor shows the vault the buy opened, read by the e2e stub's `GET /v1/portfolio` from its mock chain with `view` of `packages/basket`.
- His `/risk/*` pages stay as they are.

## 12. Agent surface

One contract, three faces. Fastify emits the OpenAPI document, committed at `packages/sdk/openapi.json`. The SDK types are generated from it and CI fails on drift. The MCP server reaches the API only through the SDK.

- **REST `/v1`:** keyless reads, and plans made from a link (gate `AGENT-LINK`); an order is created with the owner's sign-in. Amounts are strings. No agent API keys in the MVP.
- **SDK:** `BasketClient`, `client.execute(order, signers, consents)`, `verifyOrder(order, deployments)` and the guard. Plain signer callbacks, no kit or viem types in the public surface. Installed from the repo. The AGT stream builds the guard and the executor first, because the order screen depends on them.
  - As built (AGT-1): `packages/sdk` exports `guardTransaction`, `approvedSteps` (what `verifyOrder` was to be: the order as steps a transaction can be held to, or a refusal), `execute(order, deps)`, `createOrderApi(fetch)` for the five order routes, `basketIdOfPlan`, and the two vault-address rules. It has no dependency: SHA-256, Keccak-256, the Ed25519 curve test, base58 and the ABI codec are in the package, each held by a test to Node, viem or `@solana/kit`. It imports the shared types as types only, so `pnpm --filter @colosseum/sdk build` gives plain JavaScript with no workspace code in it. AGT-2 added the client and the types: `createTenonfiClient(fetch)` calls any route by its method and path (`call('GET /v1/shelf', { query })`) and Bearing's asset facts (`assetRisk`), typed by `src/api/types.ts`, written from the committed document by `scripts/gen-api-types.ts` (no dependency) and held to it by `scripts/api-types.test.ts`; `src/api/client.test.ts` holds the generated answers to the shared zod types. The guard's and executor's own declarations still name `@colosseum/schemas`.
- **MCP (`apps/mcp`):** the current spec (2026-07-28) has no handshake and no sessions **[C 10]**. `@modelcontextprotocol/server` over stateless Streamable HTTP at `/mcp`; it also answers older clients. No login. Fixable failures return `isError: true` with a code and a `fix` line. Fallback if the v2 SDK misbehaves with real clients: `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.31.0.
  - As built (AGT-2, gate `MCP-TOOLS`): `@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.3.0, one server per request (`apps/mcp/src/server.ts`), on Node's HTTP server (`http.ts`): `/mcp`, `/llms.txt`, `/openapi.json` (the committed document) and `/health`; a request from a browser origin not in `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is refused. Seven tools (`tools.ts`): `get_chains` (`GET /v1/config`), `get_portfolio` (a vault by its address, for anybody; the person's own with their sign-in), `build_plan` (`POST /v1/baskets/propose`: the plan, `approvalUrl` at `/plan/{id}` and `buyUrl`), `get_shared_portfolios` (the shelf or one by slug; the name and description moved under `untrusted`, beside the on-chain id, the creator, the platform badge and the page's link), `prepare_order` (checks the plan or the portfolio and answers the page the person signs on: `/plan/{id}/buy`, `/indexes/{slug}/buy`, or `/indexes/{slug}` for a follow), `get_order_status` (with the person's sign-in; otherwise the link), `get_asset_risk` (`GET /risk/facts/assets/{id}`). Each tool's input and output schema is cut from the OpenAPI document (`contract.ts`), and the SDK validates every answer against it. The tools reach the API only through the SDK's client (`createTenonfiClient`, typed by `src/api/types.ts`, which `pnpm --filter @colosseum/sdk api-types` writes from the document; a test fails on drift). A client may send a person's two sign-in headers; only those are passed to the API. No route that builds, reports or places an order is ever called.
  - A plan made from a link (gate `AGENT-LINK`): `POST /v1/baskets/propose` and `GET /v1/baskets/{id}`, public, while `AGENT_SURFACE` is on, with a 32 KB body, themes only as shelf slugs, 500 a day across every caller together (one caller can use up the day) and an unbought one deleted after 7 days (`LINKED_PLANS` in `routes/v1/baskets.ts`). An order stores its vault number (`Order.basketId`, migration 0015); a step is built for it, and refused when the plan is gone; the runner takes it from the order when it is the plan's or the person's own number (`planNumberOf`), and refuses any other. The plan screen reads such a plan from the API when the tab has none and says it came from a link; the order record keeps `linked`, and the runner, the guard's terms and the portfolio's join number the vault `basketIdOfLinkedPlan(plan id, user id)`, as the API does (`basketIdOfLinked`), so the link does not lead to a buyer's vault.
- **Skill and docs:** `skills/tenonfi/SKILL.md` and `llms.txt`, which the MCP server serves with its deployment's own addresses. Fetch live data and never invent an asset or a number; propose, then sign; match a shared portfolio by id, never by name; its text is untrusted; units; error codes; the disclaimer each answer carries.

| How an agent gets authority | Who signs | What bounds it |
|---|---|---|
| Propose, person signs | The owner, at `approvalUrl` | The review screen and the guard; consents need their own tap |
| Agent-run shared portfolio | The follower once; then the keeper | Creator limits, the 48-hour delay, every vault check |
| Agent's own vault | The agent's wallet | Only what was deposited there |

Abuse limits. Portfolio names and descriptions: 280 characters, links stripped, returned in a field named `untrusted`. An order cannot switch auto-follow on or accept a new asset by itself. `agentLabel` is shown as "unverified". A daily budget on the model; past it, the regex parser answers. A plan's quotes are all on its chain; they run in parallel with 8 seconds for the chain, from the cache where they can.

## 13. Security model and tests before the submission

The keeper is the bounded risk: a leaked keeper key can cost each auto-follow vault at most 2% of the vault in any seven days (twice the weekly cap parameter, section 5) plus any error in the price reference, which on Solana is held inside the range the admin gave each asset. The upgrade key is the unbounded one: it can replace vault code, and there is no deposit cap. The API cannot sign, and it cannot make the keeper sign: the keeper plans from chain state and never reads a job the API wrote.

| Key | Power | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Deployer | Deploy and first config, then nothing | Fresh per chain; an encrypted keystore with a password prompt. Hands admin to the admin key in the same session |
| Admin / upgrade | Replace vault code, set config and caps, unpause, loosen a halt, rotate the guardian | One disclosed key per chain for now, in a password-protected keystore on a founder's machine; the app says who holds it. Squads on Solana and Safe on the EVM chains after the MVP |
| Guardian | Pause, halt an asset, add a closed day, veto a version. Tighten only | A password-protected keystore on each founder's machine. Never in the keeper's store |
| Keeper | One function | The keeper machine, gas only, under $20 |
| Platform creator | Publishes the launch portfolios | A gas-only key; its address earns the platform badge |

No mainnet key that can move funds or loosen a limit sits where a coding agent has a shell. On test networks an agent may hold the deploy keys (decided on Oct 2); they are kept outside the repo. `scripts/ops/authority-check.ts` compares the live admin, beacon owner, implementation slots, guardian, keeper, `launched` and the publish delay on three chains with `deployments/*.json`, whose expected values are written before the deploy. It runs daily in demo week.

**Hostile cases.** Frozen on day 1. The same ID names a test on each chain family; `docs/vault/SECURITY.md` holds the matrix.

- A1 output sent to the keeper. A2 price worse than tolerance. A3 back-and-forth churn. A4 token outside the accepted recipe.
- A5 stale price. A5b another asset's price at the pinned Scope index (Solana; must revert once `source_check` is on).
- A6 Saturday, and one minute before the open. A6b a weekday holiday with a feed under 26 hours old.
- A7 wrong direction or past target. A8 paused: keeper reverts, owner withdraws.
- A9 one token frozen or returning `false`: the rest withdraw. A9b a transfer hook added after deposit (Solana).
- A10 balance changed from outside, including a second token account owned by the vault.
- A11 any approval, Permit2 allowance, delegate or close authority left after a call. A12 inside the multiplier window.
- A13 caller not the keeper, or auto-follow off. A14 new asset not accepted by the owner.
- A15 upgrade to a dummy v2 with the admin key: state intact, withdraw works; `initialize` reverts on every logic contract and live proxy.
- A16 creator limits, from the shared vectors.
- A17 a router or hooked pool re-enters `adoptVersion`, `multicall` or `keeperSwap` mid-swap.
- A18 a create or accept signed against version N lands after N+1 is active.

**Invariants.** I1 tokens leave only by an owner call, and only to the owner. I2 value at reference prices after any keeper sequence in 7 days ≥ start × (1 − 2 × cap), net of owner flows, and ≥ start × (1 − cap) for losses taken at once: the weekly counter drains as it fills (section 5). With the cap at its start of 100 bps that is 2%. I3 no allowance survives a call, Permit2 included. I4 withdraw works with feeds, registry and factory reverting. I5 a keeper call never widens distance from target: the program and the contracts check it for the asset the leg trades (`check_no_further` on Solana, `_noFurther` on EVM); the other assets move only by what the leg lost as a share of the vault, which the tolerance and the cap bound.

**Gate `G-SEC`, per chain, in two tiers.** Auto-follow goes live on a chain only when tier 1 passes; until then the app hides the switch. A chain that misses on Oct 8 ships owner-signed only.

| Tier 1: required to switch auto-follow on | Passing |
|---|---|
| Every A-case that applies, as a named test (Foundry at 6, 8 and 18 decimals; LiteSVM under Vitest with `mock-router` as `Config.router_program` and a hand-written Scope account) | All pass |
| Each rule bites: comment out each check in turn, in the contracts and in `checks.rs` | A named test fails for each |
| I1 and I4 | Hold in unit tests and on live state |
| One scripted mainnet rehearsal, $10 to $20, every transaction logged | Create, deposit, owner swap, publish, adopt, one keeper leg; A1, A2, A4, A8 and A13 fail as expected on live state; pause; withdraw while paused |
| The keeper key holds only gas, sits in a password-protected keystore no coding agent can read, and is in no repository secret | Checked by a person |

| Tier 2: run and recorded; open items go in `docs/vault/SECURITY.md` and the trust notice | Tool |
|---|---|
| EVM invariants I2, I3, I5 with handlers (owner, hostile keeper, creator, donor, issuer, clock, a re-entering router) | Foundry |
| Solana logic and sequences | `proptest` on `checks.rs`; 10,000 fast-check sequences on LiteSVM |
| A1 and A11 against a real `route_v2` account list; a saved route replays | A local validator with mainnet's Jupiter and pool (`programs/tests/jupiter-replay.ts`): done for the owner's swap on Oct 3. surfpool is not installed |
| Static analysis | Slither, Aderyn, `forge lint`, `forge build --sizes`, `cargo clippy`, cargo-deny |
| Robinhood fork at a pinned block, including a Saturday block | dRPC |
| A second rehearsal, with A15 run with the admin key | Scripted |

A tier 2 failure blocks only if it shows a real way to lose funds.

**Gate `G-LINK`, once, before the public link is shared.**

- Guard negatives, one vector each: a hostile instruction in an allowed program, a withdraw built for a third party, a wrong spender, `setOperator`, auto-follow without consent, a swap whose minimum differs from the screen.
- Identity: wallet B cannot approve wallet A's order; B claiming A's address is refused; an unrelated successful transaction cannot confirm a leg.
- No registered route reaches a signer. A keeper row inserted with the `api` role is never submitted. 100 anonymous quote calls cause a bounded number of upstream calls.
- `authority-check` is green with the disclosed admin key as admin, `launched` true and the delay at 172,800 s. The Supabase Data API is off.
- The three-profile test passes: pairwise distance at least 3,000 bps, a reason on every line.

**Supply chain.** Keep pnpm 11's one-day release hold and build-script approval. A pull request that changes the lockfile names the new packages and a person reads the diff. `security.yml` runs gitleaks, `pnpm audit --prod` and cargo-deny, on every pull request and weekly. The audit fails on a high or critical advisory that is not listed, with its reason, in `docs/vault/SECURITY-DEPS.md`. One advisory is left failing on purpose, `braces` (GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm): a pull request may merge while it is the audit's only finding (gate `AUDIT-BRACES`).

**First, today.** Revoke the two live approvals on Rodrigo's demo wallet (5 USDY and 5 syrupUSDC to the agent key). Do not deploy the current API publicly with `secrets/agent.json` present.

**What the app says,** from one `TRUST_STATUS` constant: unaudited; the team holds the upgrade keys and could move funds; what the keeper may do, in numbers, plus any error in the price reference; issuers can pause, freeze or seize; not advice; the open tier 2 items.

**Incident plan** (`docs/vault/INCIDENT.md`): `pnpm ops:pause` on three chains in one command, stop the keeper, set the banner, rotate the keeper key. Rehearse it once on mainnet: alert to paused in under 5 minutes.

## 14. Roadmap seams

| Roadmap item | In place at the MVP | Added later |
|---|---|---|
| Community, gamification | Same events on both families; `follows`; `creator_user_id`; value and counts from `vaults`; a `badges` slot on cards | Profiles, rankings, an event index |
| Creator fees | `maxFeeBps` and `flags` stored at publish on both families and required to be zero; `Order.fees`; nullable `fee_bps`, `fee_amount` | Accrual through an upgrade |
| Publish once (CCIP) | `familyId` onchain on all three chains; chain-free EVM portfolio ids; publish is already an order with a leg per chain | An authorized remote publisher; one leg |
| More chains | `chains` rows and `content/chains.json` are the only chain lists; init in the proxy constructor, CREATE2; a `Submitter` per chain | One config, one deploy. A third wallet family would touch `Owner` and `WalletPort` |
| New plan types | Targets stored in the vault, separate from the recipe; a vault type byte; `basketType` on the sheet; `kind` and `params` on families | A protected plan is an upgrade that adds a target rule to check 5, plus fields and a sleeve rule |
| Rebalance on drift | The keeper path needs auto-follow and stored targets, not a recipe or a new version; `listAutoFollowVaults()` takes no recipe; `Leg.trigger` has `drift` | The keeper pass without its `synced_version` step, and the switch shown on plans |
| Paid price feeds | On Solana: `price_kind`, four price-account slots, and a 32-byte field plus 21 spare bytes per asset (a Pyth feed id is 32 bytes). On EVM: `source` in `AssetConfig`. `Sourced` on every price | A second source or a cross-check |
| Agent-run portfolios | A creator is an address; `creator_kind`; a per-vault `keeper` / `operator` slot | A per-vault agent operator under the same checks; agent API keys |
| Pooled token | The vault owner may be a program or a contract; no `tx.origin`; `vault_type` | A separate program reading the same recipes |
| Embeds | Every write goes through `/v1`; `WalletPort` has no Privy types; nullable `org_id` on orders; an `(embed)` route group | Partner keys, OAuth, `createVaultFor` |
| Audits, governance | One admin address with two-step transfer; `deployments/*.json`; parameter bounds in code; the invariant suite | A multisig, a timelock, an audit, verified builds |

## 15. Workstreams

Only the TypeScript streams wait for PR1a. `SOL`, `EVM`, `RISK`, `BRAND`, `OPS`, the Privy page and the personalization port import nothing from it and start now, while PR0 and PR1a are written.

| ID | Stream | Owner | Inputs | Outputs | Depends on | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FRAME | PR0, PR1a, PR1b | Thom | Sections 2 to 4 | Green CI, v0 types, mock adapter, migration `0006`, flags; then boundary test and workflows | Rodrigo's answers on branch and licence | The walking skeleton passes on the mock |
| SOL | Solana program | Thom | 3.7, 5, the spike | `programs/basket`, IDL, LiteSVM suite | none | All A-cases pass; the $10 run passes |
| EVM | Contracts | Thom | 3.8, 5, 6, the spike | `contracts/`, ABIs, deploy scripts, `deployments/*.json` | none | Unit and fuzz tests pass; deployed and verified on both chains |
| ADS | Solana adapter | Thom | 3.2, the IDL | Vault builders on kit 2.3; Scope and multiplier reads; holdings for both token programs; `send.ts`, `track` | FRAME, SOL's IDL | Contract tests pass on a fork, then on mainnet with small amounts |
| ADE | EVM adapter | Thom | 3.2, ABIs | One codebase, a config per chain; quoters; router calldata | FRAME, EVM's ABIs | Same, on both chains |
| BAS | `packages/basket` | Thom | 3.6, 6 | Flatten, view, planner, limit check, roll-up, meta hash, shared limit vectors | FRAME | Unit and property tests pass; the limit check matches both registries on the vectors |
| API | Backend | Thom | 3.3, 3.4, 4, 10 | Auth, limits, `/v1` routes, `prepareIntent`, funding, report matching, OpenAPI emit | FRAME | Endpoint and security tests pass |
| KEEP | Keeper | Thom | 3.5, 10 | `apps/keeper`, alerts, the run script for the keeper machine | BAS, ADS, ADE | A shared-portfolio update rebalances an auto-follow vault on each live chain; two sessions of clean runs |
| WAL | Sign-in | Thom | 3.5, 9 | Privy provider, `WalletPort`, test wallet | The Privy app and origin | On each chain, a passkey wallet signs; `next build` passes |
| AGT | Agent surface | Thom | 9, 12 | Guard and executor first; then `packages/sdk`, `apps/mcp`, skill, `llms.txt` | FRAME; API for the rest | Guard negatives pass; an outside agent builds a plan and a person approves it from the link |
| WEB | Screens | Thom, on Rodrigo's design system | 11 | His three screens extended, then the new ones, one agent per screen | BRAND's primitives, WAL, AGT's executor, API (mock first) | The nine MVP items work end to end; the seven binding rules hold; axe passes at 375 px in light and dark |
| ENG | Personalization | Thom's agent ports; Rodrigo owns the table and wording | 3.6, 7, the prototype | `engine/src/personal/`, the parameter table, templates, a 12-goal eval | BAS's `flatten` | The three-profile test passes; his baseline test stays green |
| RISK | Risk layer, sheets | Rodrigo | 8 | Three fixes; about ten family sheets; a dated dump; six lines in `compute.ts` | none | Sheets render; hosted curves show their date |
| REVM | EVM depth | Thom | 8 | The hourly collector | RISK's `compute.ts` change | Curves for five tokens per EVM chain, 8 samples per regime |

| OPS | Mainnet work a person does | Thom; Rodrigo is asked to take the Solana sessions | 10, 13, 16 | `docs/vault/RUNBOOK-OPS.md`: a checklist per session with a name per item. Hosting, accounts, admin keys, funded wallets, deploys, config, seeding, rehearsals, footage | Accounts opened | The public URL serves the app; `authority-check` is green |
| BRAND | Design system in the app | Rodrigo | `.design/branding/working-brand/patterns/` | Tokens and fonts in `globals.css`, the primitives in `components/ui/`, route groups, his three screens on them; the final logo artwork | none | The primitives exist and his screens render on them in light and dark |

OPS sets the pace and is planned like a stream. It is three $10 runs, three deploys, about 50 asset entries, about 13 recipe publishes, three admin keys, funded wallets, two rehearsals, a pause drill and the footage, mostly inside five sessions of 5.5 hours. Every `scripts/ops/*` script is idempotent, has a dry run that prints the exact transactions, and reads one JSON file of assets, feeds and recipes. The deployer writes config in the deploy session; later changes go through the admin key as one batch.

## 16. Day-by-day plan and what is out

The schedule of record is `PLAN-VAULT.md`, written on the night of Oct 1 with the streams not yet started: it moves the Oct 1 row below into Oct 2 and adds dated milestones. Where the two differ, the plan wins. The table below is kept for its reasoning.

Stock markets are closed on Oct 3, 4, 10 and 11. Keeper trades on stocks, and any footage of them, can only happen on Oct 2 and Oct 5 to 9, between 14:30 and 20:00 UTC (11:30 to 17:00 BRT). Until `launch()` the publish delay is 300 s, so one session holds several publish, adopt and trade cycles; each cycle uses a fresh test portfolio and vault with a few dollars of team money. Oct 12 is a US trading day (memory).

| Day | What happens |
|---|---|
| Thu Oct 1 | SOL, EVM, the personalization port and the Privy page start from sections 3.7, 3.8 and 7. Accounts opened |
| Fri Oct 2 | PR0 and PR1a by midday; v0 interfaces. In session, and nothing else for Thom: the three $10 runs and the Scope read. All three chains funded, so a failure to get USDG on chain 4663 shows on day 1. Second Jupiter organisation. Privy origin test. Half-day tries: one mock MCP tool on the free host, Slither on the spike vault, one hand-written kit 2.3 builder. Rodrigo answers section 17 |
| Sat Oct 3 | SOL: `route_v2` on surfpool, registry, create and first-buy sizes at 7 and 12 assets. EVM: vault, factory, registry with unit tests. Adapters: read side. API: auth and orders. AGT: guard and executor. WEB: shared layer. Three-profile test green on placeholder numbers. Log feed ages over the weekend |
| Sun Oct 4 | The keeper path in program and contracts; A-cases; adapters build and simulate on a fork. Evening: the interface amendment window closes and the hash test turns on. Origin decided. Admin keys created. Design system applied to the app by now. Deploy dry run |
| Mon Oct 5 | Deploy on three chains: config, caps, handover to the admin key, `authority-check`. First hosted web and API; cold-start test (no pings for 20 minutes, then load the home page). In session: the owner path on three chains; first keeper leg and first publish-adopt-trade cycle on Solana |
| Tue Oct 6 | A buy with a passkey wallet on each chain; publish and follow. In session: auto-follow cycles on Robinhood Chain; rehearsal 1 on Solana and Robinhood Chain. MCP against the real API |
| Wed Oct 7 | In session: rehearsal 2, and the market-open footage. Portfolio and rebalance end to end. Sheets render. Add-backs decided |
| Thu Oct 8 | `G-SEC` per chain at 12:00 BRT. Then `launch()`: the delay is 48 hours from here on. Then `G-LINK`, and only then is the link shared. Pause drill in session. In session: publish a version of the demo portfolio |
| Fri Oct 9 | Spare session for a failed test cycle; building continues, no freeze (gate `NO-FREEZE`). The version published on Oct 8 takes effect on Saturday, when the stock market is closed, so its rebalance waits for Monday |
| Sat Oct 10 | Building continues, each change with its tests. README, `HANDOFF-VAULT`, `DESIGN-VAULT`, `PRIOR-WORK`. Record the remaining screens |
| Sun Oct 11 | Edit both videos. Fill the submission form |
| Mon Oct 12 | In session: the Oct 8 version adopts and rebalances at production settings. If it fails, auto-follow is switched off on that chain. Then submit with a buffer |

**Out unless ahead.** Each item starts out and comes back only on its date, if the streams it needs are done.
